Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"errors"
6: 	"fmt"
7: 	"os"
8: 	"path/filepath"
9: 	"strings"
10: 	"sync"
11: 	"time"
12: 
13: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
14: 	clabconstants "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants"
15: 	clabnodes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes"
16: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
17: )
18: 
19: func (c *CLab) Save(
20: 	ctx context.Context,
21: 	options ...SaveOption,
22: ) error {
23: 	opts := NewSaveOptions()
24: 	for _, opt := range options {
25: 		opt(opts)
26: 	}
27: 
28: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
29: 		return c.saveWithLabRuntime(ctx, opts)
30: 	}
31: 
32: 	if opts.copyDst != "" {
33: 		resolvedDst, err := c.resolveCopyOutDst(opts.copyDst)
34: 		if err != nil {
35: 			return err
36: 		}
37: 		opts.copyDst = resolvedDst
38: 	}
39: 
40: 	var wg sync.WaitGroup
41: 	errs := make([]error, 0, len(c.Nodes))
42: 
43: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
44: 		errs = append(errs, nil)
45: 		i := len(errs) - 1
46: 
47: 		wg.Add(1)
48: 		go func(node clabnodes.Node) {
49: 			defer wg.Done()
50: 
51: 			result, err := node.SaveConfig(ctx)
52: 			if err != nil {
53: 				errs[i] = fmt.Errorf("node %q save failed: %w", node.GetShortName(), err)
54: 				return
55: 			}
56: 
57: 			if opts.copyDst == "" {
58: 				return
59: 			}
60: 
61: 			if err := c.copySavedConfig(ctx, result, node, opts.copyDst); err != nil {
62: 				errs[i] = fmt.Errorf("node %q save copy failed: %w", node.GetShortName(), err)
63: 			}
64: 		}(node)
65: 	}
66: 
67: 	wg.Wait()
68: 
69: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
70: }
71: 
72: func (c *CLab) resolveCopyOutDst(dst string) (string, error) {
73: 	baseDir, err := os.Getwd()
74: 	if err != nil || baseDir == "" {
75: 		baseDir = c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()
76: 	}
77: 	if baseDir == "" {
78: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: current working directory is empty")
79: 	}
80: 
81: 	resolvedDst := clabutils.ResolvePath(dst, baseDir)
82: 	labDir := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()
83: 	labDirName := filepath.Base(labDir)
84: 	if labDirName == "" || labDirName == "." {
85: 		if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
86: 			labDirName = "clab-" + c.Config.Name
87: 		} else {
88: 			return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: lab directory is empty")
89: 		}
90: 	}
91: 
92: 	dstLabDir := filepath.Join(resolvedDst, labDirName)
93: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(dstLabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault); err != nil {
94: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create save dst directory %q: %w", dstLabDir, err)
95: 	}
96: 
97: 	return dstLabDir, nil
98: }
99: 
100: // copySavedConfig copies the saved configuration file from the path in the SaveConfigResult
101: // to the user-specified destination directory with a timestamp in the filename.
102: // A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to point to the
103: // latest timestamped copy.
104: //
105: // The destination layout is:
106: //
107: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config-060102_150405.json   # timestamped copy
108: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config.json                 # symlink -> timestamped copy
109: func (c *CLab) copySavedConfig(
110: 	ctx context.Context,
111: 	result *clabnodes.SaveConfigResult,
112: 	node clabnodes.Node,
113: 	dstRoot string,
114: ) error {
115: 	if result == nil || result.ConfigPath == "" {
116: 		log.Debug("no saved config path reported, skipping copy", "node", node.GetShortName())
117: 		return nil
118: 	}
119: 
120: 	nodeCfg := node.Config()
121: 	if nodeCfg == nil {

159: // e.g. "config.json" with ts 2026-02-06 23:59:00 UTC -> "config-260206_235900.json".

163: 	ts := t.UTC().Format("060102_150405")

189: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces (endpoints) a node has.

1176: // ParkEndpoints moves all tracked endpoints into the node-owned parking namespace.
1177: func (d *DefaultNode) ParkEndpoints(ctx context.Context) error {
1178: 	parkPath, err := clabutils.CreateOrGetNamedNetNS(d.parkingNetNSName())
1179: 	if err != nil {
1180: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create parking netns for node %q: %w", d.Cfg.ShortName, err)
1181: 	}
1182: 	// Reuse the parking node retained by a failed restore so it keeps the parked endpoints.
1183: 	parkingNode := d.parkingNode
1184: 	reused := parkingNode != nil
1185: 	if !reused {
1186: 		parkingNode = clablinks.NewParkingNode(d.Cfg.LongName, parkPath)
1187: 	}
1188: 
1189: 	if err := parkingNode.CaptureFrom(ctx, d); err != nil {
1190: 		if !reused {
1191: 			_ = d.cleanupParkingNetNS()
1192: 		}
1193: 		return err
1194: 	}
1195: 
1196: 	if err := parkingNode.RepointSymlink(); err != nil {
1197: 		repointErr := fmt.Errorf("failed to repoint symlink for node %q: %w", d.Cfg.ShortName, err)
1198: 		if _, restoreErr := parkingNode.RestoreTo(ctx, d); restoreErr != nil {
1199: 			// Keep the parking netns and its endpoints so a later restore can recover them.
1200: 			d.parkingNode = parkingNode
1201: 			return errors.Join(repointErr, restoreErr)
1202: 		}
1203: 		d.parkingNode = nil
1204: 		if cleanupErr := d.cleanupParkingNetNS(); cleanupErr != nil {
1205: 			return errors.Join(repointErr, cleanupErr)
1206: 		}
1207: 		return repointErr
1208: 	}
1209: 	d.parkingNode = parkingNode
1210: 
1211: 	return nil
1212: }
1213: 
1214: // RestoreEndpoints tries to get the parking node to unpark its interfaces.
1215: func (d *DefaultNode) RestoreEndpoints(ctx context.Context) error {
1216: 	parkPath, err := d.parkingNetNSPath()
1217: 	if err != nil {
1218: 		// No parking netns means the node had no parked interfaces - e.g. it was
1219: 		// stopped outside of containerlab (so its interfaces were never parked) or
1220: 		// it has no dataplane links. Nothing to restore.
1221: 		d.parkingNode = nil
1222: 		log.Debugf("node %q has no parking netns, nothing to restore", d.Cfg.ShortName)
1223: 		return nil
1224: 	}
1225: 	parkingNode := d.parkingNode
1226: 	if parkingNode == nil {
1227: 		parkingNode = clablinks.NewParkingNode(d.Cfg.LongName, parkPath)
1228: 	}
1229: 
1230: 	restored, err := parkingNode.RestoreTo(ctx, d)
1231: 	if err != nil {
1232: 		return err
1233: 	}
1234: 
1235: 	for _, ep := range restored {
1236: 		log.Info("Restored link", "node", d.Cfg.ShortName, "interface", ep.GetIfaceName())
1237: 	}
1238: 
1239: 	if err := d.cleanupParkingNetNS(); err != nil {
1240: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to cleanup parking netns for node %q: %w", d.Cfg.ShortName, err)
1241: 	}
1242: 	d.parkingNode = nil
1243: 
1244: 	return nil
1245: }

1247: func (d *DefaultNode) Stop(ctx context.Context) error {
1248: 	cfg := d.Config()
1249: 
1250: 	if d.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
1251: 		log.Debugf("node %q skips lifecycle stop", cfg.ShortName)
1252: 		return nil
1253: 	}
1254: 
1255: 	status := d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx)
1256: 	switch status {
1257: 	case clabruntime.Stopped:
1258: 		log.Debugf("node %q already stopped, skipping", cfg.ShortName)
1259: 		return nil
1260: 	case clabruntime.NotFound:
1261: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q container %q not found", cfg.ShortName, cfg.LongName)
1262: 	}
1263: 
1264: 	if err := d.OverwriteNode.PreStop(ctx); err != nil {
1265: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q pre-stop hook failed: %w", cfg.ShortName, err)
1266: 	}
1267: 
1268: 	if err := d.ParkEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
1269: 		return err
1270: 	}
1271: 
1272: 	if err := d.Runtime.StopContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d.StopSignal); err != nil {
1273: 		// Docker/podman may return an error while the container is already stopped.
1274: 		// if ctr is already stopped, this is OK
1275: 		if d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.Stopped {
1276: 			log.Warnf(

1278: 				cfg.ShortName,

1282: 		}

1284: 		if restoreErr := d.RestoreEndpoints(ctx); restoreErr != nil {
1285: 			return errors.Join(

1289: 		}

1295: }

1297: // Start restarts a stopped container and restores its parked dataplane interfaces.
1298: func (d *DefaultNode) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
1299: 	cfg := d.Config()
1300: 
1301: 	if d.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
1302: 		log.Debugf("node %q skips lifecycle start", cfg.ShortName)
1303: 		return nil
1304: 	}
1305: 
1306: 	status := d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx)
1307: 
1308: 	switch status {
1309: 	case clabruntime.Running:
1310: 		log.Debugf("node %q already running, skipping", cfg.ShortName)
1311: 		return nil
1312: 	case clabruntime.NotFound:
1313: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q container %q not found", cfg.ShortName, cfg.LongName)
1314: 	}
1315:

1317: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q failed starting container: %w", cfg.ShortName, err)
1318: 	}
1319: 
1320: 	if err := d.RestoreEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
1321: 		_ = d.Runtime.StopContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d.StopSignal)
1322: 		return err
1323: 	}
1324: 
1325: 	// Re-run topology exec commands on lifecycle start to restore node-local interface config
1326: 	// (for example IP addresses added during deploy exec phase).
1327: 	execCollection := clabexec.NewExecCollection()

1330: 			"failed to run exec commands for node %q on lifecycle start: %v",
1331: 			cfg.ShortName,
1332: 			err,
1333: 		)

restoreErr != nil {\n\t\t\treturn errors.Join(\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Errorf(\"node %q failed stopping container: %w\", cfg.ShortName, err),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Errorf(\"failed to restore endpoints: %w\", restoreErr),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q failed stopping container: %w\", cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// Start restarts a stopped container and restores its parked dataplane interfaces.\nfunc (d *DefaultNode) Start(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tcfg := d.Config()\n\n\tif d.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\"node %q skips lifecycle start\", cfg.ShortName)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tstatus := d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx)\n\n\tswitch status {\n\tcase clabruntime.Running:\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\"node %q already running, skipping\", cfg.ShortName)\n\t\treturn nil\n\tcase clabruntime.NotFound:\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q container %q not found\", cfg.ShortName, cfg.LongName)\n\t}\n\n\tif _, err := d.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q failed starting container: %w\", cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tif err := d.RestoreEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\t_ = d.Runtime.StopContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d.StopSignal)\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Re-run topology exec commands on lifecycle start to restore node-local interface config\n\t// (for example IP addresses added during deploy exec phase).\n\texecCollection := clabexec.NewExecCollection()\n\tif err := d.RunExecFromConfig(ctx, execCollection); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"failed to run exec commands for node %q on lifecycle start: %v\",\n\t\t\tcfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\terr,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\texecCollection.Log()\n\n\treturn nil",

  "type": "step-start",
  "id": "prt_19c9fe95e001SuPQUmLlgqRLNN",

  "messageID": "msg_19c9fdfba001limWmfCkR1QQsW"
}
Reasoning
Now let's understand the parking mechanism and where CLAB_INTFS is set. The flow: when stopping a node, endpoints are parked (moved into a parking netns), so `d.Endpoints` becomes empty and endpoints are held by `d.parkingNode`. On `Deploy` (recreate), the env var `CLAB_INTFS` should count parked endpoints too, so vrnetlab-based nodes wait for all interfaces.

Now, important question: is `EndpointsWithParked` declared on the `clabnodes.Node` interface? linux.go and iol.go call `n.EndpointsWithParked()` — iol embeds DefaultNode? Let's check iol.go and linux.go. Also check whether other node kinds that set ClabEnvIntfs were updated (e.g., sros, vr nodes?). Grep in the snapshot.
read · completed

28: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
29: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
30: 	generateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)
31: 	nrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(nil, generateNodeAttributes, nil)
32: 
33: 	r.Register(kindnames, func() clabnodes.Node {
34: 		return new(linux)
35: 	}, nrea)
36: }
37: 
38: type linux struct {
39: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
40: }
41: 
42: func (*linux) LinkApplyMode(context.Context) clabnodes.LinkApplyMode {
43: 	return clabnodes.LinkApplyModeLive
44: }
45: 
46: func (n *linux) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
47: 	// Init DefaultNode
48: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
49: 	n.Cfg = cfg
50: 
51: 	n.StopSignal = clabtypes.SIGKILL
52: 
53: 	// linux kind uses `always` as a default restart policy
54: 	// since often they run auxiliary services that might fail because
55: 	// of the wrong configuration or other reasons.
56: 	// Usually we want those services to automatically restart.
57: 	if n.Cfg.RestartPolicy == "" {
58: 		n.Cfg.RestartPolicy = "always"
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	for _, o := range opts {
62: 		o(n)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	// make ipv6 enabled on all linux node interfaces
66: 	// but not for the nodes with host network mode, as this is not supported on gh action runners
67: 	if cfg.Sysctls != nil && n.Config().NetworkMode != "host" {
68: 		cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv6.conf.all.disable_ipv6"] = "0"
69: 	}
70: 
71: 	return nil
72: }
73: 
74: func (n *linux) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
75: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces
76: 	// Which is required by vrnetlab to determine if all configured interfaces are present
77: 	// such that the internal VM can be started with these interfaces assigned.
78: 	n.Config().Env[clabconstants.ClabEnvIntfs] = strconv.Itoa(len(n.EndpointsWithParked()))
79: 
80: 	cID, err := n.Runtime.CreateContainer(ctx, n.Cfg)
81: 	if err != nil {
82: 		return err
83: 	}
84: 	_, err = n.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cID, n)
85: 
86: 	n.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
87: 
88: 	return err
89: }
90: 
91: func (n *linux) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
92: 	log.Debugf("Running postdeploy actions for Linux '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
93: 
94: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
95: 
96: 	return nil
97: }
98: 
99: // Start re-applies the eth0 TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime recreates
100: // the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
101: // See disableEth0TxOffload for why TX offload must stay disabled on eth0.
102: func (n *linux) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
103: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
104: 		return err
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
108: 	// for them as well, as there is no container netns to adjust.
109: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
110: 		return nil
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
114: 
115: 	return nil
116: }
117: 
118: // disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.
119: // Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers
120: // that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on
121: // deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.
122: //
123: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
124: // and toggling its offload settings would alter the host networking state.
125: func (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
126: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
127: 		return
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0"))
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		log.Error(err)
133: 	}
134: }
135: 
136: func (n *linux) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string {
137: 	images := make(map[string]string)
138: 	images[clabnodes.ImageKey] = n.Cfg.Image
139: 
140: 	return images
141: }
142: 
143: // CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks
144: // if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.
145: func (n *linux) CheckInterfaceName() error {
146: 	nm := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NetworkMode)
147: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
148: 		if e.GetIfaceName() == "eth0" && nm != "none" {
149: 			return fmt.Errorf(
150: 				"eth0 interface name is not allowed for %s node when network mode is not set to none",
151: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
152: 			)
153: 		}
154: 	}
155: 	return nil
156: }

err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix\n\t// for them as well, as there is no container netns to adjust.\n\tif n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tn.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.\n// Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers\n// that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on\n// deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.\n//\n// Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,\n// and toggling its offload settings would alter the host networking state.\nfunc (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {\n\tif n.Cfg.NetworkMode == \"host\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\terr := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), \"eth0\"))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Error(err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (n *linux) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string {\n\timages := make(map[string]string)\n\timages[clabnodes.ImageKey] = n.Cfg.Image\n\n\treturn images\n}\n\n// CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks\n// if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.\nfunc (n *linux) CheckInterfaceName() error {\n\tnm := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NetworkMode)\n\tfor _, e := range n.Endpoints {\n\t\tif e.GetIfaceName() == \"eth0\" && nm != \"none\" {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"eth0 interface name is not allowed for %s node when network mode is not set to none\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}",

202: // swapMgmtIdx swaps linux interface index 0 with the management interface's index,
203: // so Ethernet0/0 takes the index a relocated management interface vacated.
204: func (n *iol) swapMgmtIdx(idx int) int {
205: 	switch idx {
206: 	case 0:
207: 		return n.mgmtLinuxIdx
208: 	case n.mgmtLinuxIdx:
209: 		return 0
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	return idx
213: }
214: 
215: // iolPortForLinuxIdx returns the IOL slot/port backed by linux interface index idx.
216: func (n *iol) iolPortForLinuxIdx(idx int) (slot, port int) {
217: 	mapped := n.swapMgmtIdx(idx)
218: 	return mapped / 4, mapped % 4
219: }
220: 
221: // ensureNumSlotsEnv exports CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS when the management interface is
222: // relocated, so that its slot exists even when no link lands in it.
223: func (n *iol) ensureNumSlotsEnv() {
224: 	if n.mgmtLinuxIdx == 0 || n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != "" {
225: 		return
226: 	}
227: 
228: 	intfIdxRegexp := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
229: 	needed := n.mgmtSlot + 1
230: 
231: 	for _, ep := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
232: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName()))
233: 		if slot, _ := n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x); slot+1 > needed {
234: 			needed = slot + 1
235: 		}
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] = strconv.Itoa(needed)
239: }

267:

282: // Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime
283: // recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
284: // See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.
285: func (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
286: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
287: 		return err
288: 	}
289: 
290: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
291: 	// for them as well, as there is no container mgmt veth to adjust.
292: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
293: 		return nil
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
297: 
298: 	return nil
299: }
300: 
301: // disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.
302: // iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded
303: // (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its
304: // TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.
305: //
306: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
307: // and there is no clab-created mgmt veth to adjust.
308: func (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
309: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
310: 		return
311: 	}
312: 
313: 	if n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
314: 		return
315: 	}
316: 
317: 	var peerIfIndex int
318: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex("eth0", &peerIfIndex))
319: 	if err != nil {
320: 		log.Warn("Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface",
321: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
322: 			"error", err)
323: 		return
324: 	}
325: 
326: 	if err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {
327: 		log.Warn("Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth",
328: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
329: 			"error", err)
330: 	}
331: }

333: func (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {
334: 	// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create
335: 	// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.
336: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {
337: 		// create nvram file

344: 		clabutils.CreateFile(bootCfg, "")
345: 	}
346: 
347: 	return n.GenInterfaceConfig(ctx)
348: }

350: // Generate interfaces configuration for IOL (and iouyap/netmap).
351: func (n *iol) GenInterfaceConfig(_ context.Context) error {
352: 	// add 'boilerplate' to NETMAP and iouyap.ini for the management port
353: 	iouyapData := fmt.Sprintf(
354: 		"[default]\nbase_port = 49000\nnetmap = /iol/NETMAP\n[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = eth0\n",
355: 		n.mgmtSlot,
356: 		n.mgmtPort,
357: 	)
358: 	netmapdata := fmt.Sprintf(
359: 		"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n",
360: 		n.Pid,
361: 		n.mgmtSlot,

363: 		n.mgmtSlot,
364: 		n.mgmtPort,
365: 	)
366: 
367: 	slot, port := 0, 0
368: 
369: 	// Regexp to pull number out of linux'ethX' interface naming
370: 	IntfRegExpr := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
371: 
372: 	for _, intf := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
373: 		// get numeric interface number and cast to int
374: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(IntfRegExpr.FindString(intf.GetIfaceName()))
375: 
376: 		// Interface naming is Ethernet{slot}/{port}. Each slot contains max 4 ports
377: 		slot, port = n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)
378: 
379: 		// append data to write to NETMAP and IOUYAP files
380: 		iouyapData += fmt.Sprintf("[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = %s\n", slot, port, intf.GetIfaceName())
381: 		netmapdata += fmt.Sprintf("%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n", n.Pid, slot, port, slot, port)
382: 
383: 		// populate template array for config
384: 		ipv4Addr := ""
385: 		ipv4Mask := ""
386: 		v4Prefix := intf.GetIPv4Addr()
387: 		ipv6Addr := ""
388: 
389: 		if v4Prefix.IsValid() {
390: 			ipv4Addr = v4Prefix.Addr().String()
391: 			ipv4Mask = clabutils.CIDRToDDN(v4Prefix.Bits())
392: 		}
393: 
394: 		if a := intf.GetIPv6Addr(); a.IsValid() {
395: 			ipv6Addr = a.String()
396: 		}
397: 
398: 		n.interfaces = append(n.interfaces,
399: 			IOLInterface{
400: 				IfaceName: intf.GetIfaceName(),
401: 				IfaceIdx:  x,
402: 				Slot:      slot,
403: 				Port:      port,
404: 				IPv4Addr:  ipv4Addr,
405: 				IPv4Mask:  ipv4Mask,
406: 				IPv6Addr:  ipv6Addr,
407: 			},
408: 		)
409: 	}

414: 		return err
415: 	}

417: 
418: 	return err
419: }

164: func (p *ParkingNode) captureCandidates(
165: 	ctx context.Context,
166: 	src Node,

198: func trackedIfaceNames(endpointSets ...[]Endpoint) map[string]struct{} {
199: 	ifaceNames := map[string]struct{}{}
200: 
201: 	for _, endpoints := range endpointSets {
202: 		for _, ep := range endpoints {
203: 			ifaceNames[ep.GetIfaceName()] = struct{}{}
204: 		}
205: 	}
206: 
207: 	return ifaceNames
208: }
209: 
210: func listOwnedInterfaceNames(
211: 	ctx context.Context,
212: 	node Node,
213: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
214: ) ([]string, error) {
215: 	var ifaces []string
216: 
217: 	err := node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
218: 		links, err := netlink.LinkList()
219: 		if err != nil {
220: 			return err
221: 		}
222: 
223: 		for _, link := range links {
224: 			if !isOwnedInterface(link, knownIfaceNames) {
225: 				continue
226: 			}
227: 
228: 			ifaces = append(ifaces, link.Attrs().Name)
229: 		}
230: 
231: 		return nil
232: 	})
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return nil, err
235: 	}
236: 
237: 	slices.Sort(ifaces)
238: 
239: 	return ifaces, nil
240: }
241: 
242: func isOwnedInterface(link netlink.Link, knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{}) bool {
243: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
244: 	switch name {
245: 	case "lo", "eth0":
246: 		return false
247: 	}
248: 
249: 	if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known && !hasOwnershipAltName(link) {
250: 		return false
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	return true
254: }

Reasoning
The `cmd/save.go` returns the error from `c.Save` — so previously `clab save` exited 0 even on failures; now it will exit non-zero. That's a behavior change but likely intended improvement. Check `Save` doc or usage in api-server: `core/labruntime.go` line 401 uses `c.LabRuntime.Save` (different path, the srsim runtime). Only two callers. `cmd/save.go` returns the error — that makes `clab save` fail with error. Behavior change is intended improvement (errors surfaced). Might be worth noting but not a defect.

Reasoning
Now, when is a node recreated while its endpoints are parked? Look at apply flow: nodes in `recreatedNodeSet` whose container has joinable netns get parked. So `planParkedNodes` parks nodes that will be recreated. Then lifecycle.go parks/restores. Let me read lifecycle.go lines ~30-90 to understand order: park, recreate, restore.

Reasoning
So the apply recreate flow: park endpoints (so `d.Endpoints` is emptied and `d.parkingNode` holds them), recreate the container (Deploy → CLAB_INTFS via EndpointsWithParked), then restore endpoints (move from parking netns into new container's netns). This makes sense: for vrnetlab-based images (CLAB_INTFS), and for IOL, the NETMAP/iouyap.ini files need entries for the data interfaces so iouyap can bridge them when they reappear.

Wait — but for IOL, when recreating, does PreDeploy get called again (CreateIOLFiles → GenInterfaceConfig with parked endpoints)? Yes — `deployNode` calls PreDeploy then Deploy. So during apply recreate of an IOL node whose endpoints are parked, PreDeploy runs GenInterfaceConfig over `EndpointsWithParked()`. Before this PR, it would iterate `n.Endpoints` which is empty (they're parked), so NETMAP would only have the mgmt entry — iouyap wouldn't bridge data interfaces even after restore. So this PR fixes that. Good.

Now, potential issue: `n.interfaces` accumulation across repeated GenInterfaceConfig calls. Pre-existing, but wait — is it? `n.interfaces` is used in `GenBootConfig` template (DataIFaces). Each GenInterfaceConfig appends. Is GenInterfaceConfig called multiple times in one process? CreateIOLFiles is called from PreDeploy. On `clab deploy` (fresh process), once. In apply flow, recreated IOL node: PreDeploy called once per apply. But multiple applies in one process? `clab apply` is a single command. However, `deployNode` could be called again... e.g., redeploys within one process? Let me check where else PreDeploy is called — maybe in restart flows or redeploy command. Actually `clab redeploy` = destroy + deploy (subprocess or same process?). Redeploy likely runs destroy+deploy in-process. Then PreDeploy would be called twice for the same node object? No — deploy creates new node objects via the registry. Actually `c.Nodes` are built fresh per command in NewContainerLab? During `redeploy`, nodes are re-created. Hmm, but the apply flow may call deployNode multiple times in one process for added nodes — each is a new node instance.

Pre-existing anyway; the PR didn't change the append semantics. Skip unless worsened: EndpointsWithParked adds parked endpoints — not a duplicate risk.

Now the more interesting question: are there OTHER consumers that need EndpointsWithParked but weren't updated? The PR is about ensuring parked endpoints are accounted for in pre-boot config. Consumers iterating `n.Endpoints` where parked endpoints matter:

1. `iol.CheckInterfaceName` — validation, not needed at park time.
2. `iol.Deploy`? IOL uses DefaultNode.Deploy — updated.
3. IOL GenInterfaceConfig — updated.
4. `ensureNumSlotsEnv` — updated.
5. What about the linux kind's `PostDeploy`/other? Not relevant.
6. What about other vrnetlab-based kinds that embed DefaultNode and rely on DefaultNode.Deploy for CLAB_INTFS — they're covered by the DefaultNode change.

But: does `EndpointsWithParked` get called anywhere else that could double-count? E.g., if endpoints were both in d.Endpoints and parkingNode (shouldn't happen — CaptureFrom moves ownership: `ep.SetNode(p); p.AdoptEndpoint(ep)` — and does the src release the endpoint? In `captureCandidates`, endpoints are from `src.GetEndpoints()` tracked map; in CaptureFrom, `moveEndpoint(ctx, ep, p)` — does moveEndpoint transfer ownership (ReleaseEndpoint from src)? Let me check moveEndpoint. If CaptureFrom does NOT remove from src.Endpoints, then EndpointsWithParked would double-count. Let me check moveEndpoint and GenericLinkNode.AdoptEndpoint.

Reasoning
Ownership transfer confirmed: `moveEndpoint` releases from src and adopts into dst. So parked endpoints are exclusively in `parkingNode.Endpoints`, and `d.Endpoints` is empty while parked. `EndpointsWithParked` correctly unions. No double count.

Now, are there OTHER node kinds that override `Deploy` and set CLAB_INTFS with `GetEndpoints()`? We grepped ClabEnvIntfs — only default_node and linux in head (plus tests/scripts). Good.

Now what about the `VRNetlab`-style kinds that override Deploy (e.g., sros, vr-*)? They embed DefaultNode and probably call `d.DefaultNode.Deploy(ctx, params)` — so covered. Fine.

Now consider correctness of `EndpointsWithParked` semantics: `slices.Clone(d.Endpoints)` then append parked. But wait — the clone copies `d.Endpoints` slice header; if `d.Endpoints` is nil, `slices.Clone(nil)` returns nil; append works. OK.

Alias check: when `parkingNode == nil`, returns `d.Endpoints` directly (aliased). Callers only read length. Fine.

Concurrency: EndpointsWithParked called during deploy of a node; parkingNode mutation happens in Park/Restore on same node. Deploy and park/restore aren't concurrent for the same node in the flows we saw (park before recreate, restore after). But `d.parkingNode` field is read without lock; `statemutex` exists for state only. Pre-existing pattern. OK.

Now the test: `TestDefaultNodeEndpointsWithParked`. It uses `d.parkingNode = clablinks.NewParkingNode(d.Cfg.LongName, "/run/netns/test")` and `d.parkingNode.AddEndpoint(veth(d.parkingNode, iface))`. Check `NewEndpointGeneric(n, iface, nil)` signature and `NewEndpointVeth`. Also `AdoptEndpoint` on DefaultNode requires `e.GetNode().GetShortName() == d.Cfg.ShortName`. In test, `veth(d, iface)` — endpoint node is d. OK.

One test concern: in the "all parked for recreate" case, `owned: nil` — then `d.Endpoints` is nil; `slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)` on nil returns a new non-nil slice with len 0? `slices.Grow(nil, 4)` returns a slice of length 0, cap >= 4, non-nil? Actually Grow on nil with n>0 allocates. Then EndpointsWithParked with parkingNode != nil does `slices.Clone(d.Endpoints)` — clone of empty non-nil slice returns empty non-nil; append parked — fine.

The alias check: `&got[0] == &d.Endpoints[0]` — with `len(d.Endpoints) > 0` guard. Good.

The test writes to a parking node with nsPath "/run/netns/test" but never calls ExecFunction etc. — pure data structure. Good, runnable, no root needed.

Now check the interface: is `EndpointsWithParked` part of the `clabnodes.Node` interface? Probably not — grep nodes/link.go for the Node interface. If it's not in the interface, then node kinds that embed DefaultNode get it via promotion. Fine.

Now let me check `iol.go` diff hunk 3: `CreateIOLFiles` boot_config change. Context: the comment says "create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically make folders." Note typo "monut" — pre-existing (the diff shows context line "monut" unchanged? The diff removed a blank line before the comment. Actually diff: line 252-254 context "  // create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically" — context, pre-existing typo. Not this PR's concern (surgical changes rule).

Behavior: previously, every PreDeploy truncates boot_config.txt to empty. Now only create if missing. Where is boot_config.txt consumed? It's bind-mounted to /iol/config.txt (line 167: `fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"), ":/iol/config.txt")`). GenBootConfig writes to `n.Cfg.LabDir/boot_config.txt` (line 492). PostDeploy calls GenBootConfig. So on every deploy, PreDeploy creates empty then PostDeploy overwrites with real boot config. With the change: on redeploy/recreate, boot_config.txt retains the old generated content between PreDeploy and PostDeploy. Does that matter? The container isn't running during PreDeploy (it's being recreated), so the file content during that window is irrelevant. After PostDeploy, GenBootConfig overwrites. Hmm — but wait: in `PostDeploy`, GenBootConfig is called — always. So the change only affects the window when the container is not running. Is there any scenario where the container is running while PreDeploy runs? PreDeploy is called before Deploy (create container). For an already-running container in apply flow — apply doesn't call deployNode for existing running nodes (only recreate). For recreated nodes, container is removed first? Park → recreate. During recreate: destroy old container, PreDeploy, Deploy new. File content irrelevant while no container.

Why was this change made then? Possibly because on `apply` with recreate, iol node's `firstBoot` logic: `if !FileExists(nvram) { firstBoot = true }`. If boot_config.txt was truncated... hmm. Actually maybe the reason: CreateFile with "" would truncate boot_config.txt, and if PostDeploy's GenBootConfig is skipped or fails, the node boots with an empty config.txt, losing config. But GenBootConfig is unconditional in PostDeploy.

Wait — actually look again: is there a scenario where PreDeploy runs without a subsequent PostDeploy GenBootConfig? `deployNode`: PreDeploy → Deploy → UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo. PostDeploy is called separately later (postdeploy phase). If deploy fails after PreDeploy but before PostDeploy, previously boot_config.txt was wiped; on next successful start (e.g., `clab start`?), the container would boot with empty config. With the change, old config is preserved. This is a resilience improvement. Fine — harmless.

Hmm, but is it needed? The change is tiny. OK.

Now — the docs: verify the telnet claim vs implementation. The IOL console: does containerlab expose port 5000? Check iol.go for port publishing / longnames. Grep "5000" in head.

Reasoning
The `telnet <container-name/id> 5000` pattern is already established in other kinds docs (e.g., `dell_sonic.md`, `plvision_sonic.md`, `sonic-vm.md`) — so it's a convention presumably supported by the environment (container name resolution works on the mgmt bridge? Actually containerlab kind docs use this pattern, so it's accepted convention in this repo). Good — consistent.

But one difference: the new IOL doc says "The console is served on the node's management address" — fine.

Now, "after November 2026" — today's date is Nov 2, 2026. Fine.

Now check the `n.mgmtLinuxIdx` / `ensureNumSlotsEnv` interplay with parked endpoints: `ensureNumSlotsEnv` iterates EndpointsWithParked, computing needed slots from iface index. Parked endpoints keep their iface names, so slots computed correctly. But careful: `iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)` uses `swapMgmtIdx` which uses `n.mgmtLinuxIdx`. Fine.

Wait — is there an issue with parked endpoint interface names in `GenInterfaceConfig` when the parked endpoint belongs to the parking node? `intf.GetIfaceName()` is fine. The NETMAP maps slot/port → eth_dev = ethX inside the IOL container. The parked endpoints will later be restored into the container netns with the same names. Good.

Now check `n.interfaces` again: with parked endpoints included during a recreate (apply flow), `n.interfaces` gets entries for parked endpoints. GenBootConfig template DataIFaces — used to generate interface config (IP addresses) for IOL. Hmm wait, let me look at how n.interfaces is used — it's used in the boot config template to configure IP addresses on interfaces. With parked endpoints included, IPs get configured. Before the PR, on recreate, parked endpoints would be omitted → IOL boots with fewer interfaces configured → after restore, data interfaces lack IPs. So the PR fixes that. Good.

But wait — subtle: `GenInterfaceConfig` is called by `CreateIOLFiles` in PreDeploy. `n.interfaces` accumulates across calls (no reset). If PreDeploy is called twice on the same node instance (e.g., apply flow that deploys an added node and then... no, added nodes deployed once). Redeploy = destroy+deploy; are node objects reused? In `clab redeploy`, it's likely `destroy` then `deploy` in-process — nodes get rebuilt in NewContainerLab for deploy. Probably fresh objects. Pre-existing anyway.

Now — is there a duplicate risk introduced by EndpointsWithParked being called in GenInterfaceConfig while some endpoints appear both in `n.Endpoints` AND parking node? Established: ownership transfer prevents that.

Edge case: `RestoreEndpoints` failure keeps `d.parkingNode = parkingNode` (line 1200 path is for ParkEndpoints' repoint failure). In RestoreEndpoints error path (line 1230-1233), `d.parkingNode` retains the parking node if it existed, or stays nil if it was created fresh but RestoreTo failed... wait: if `d.parkingNode == nil` and parkingNode was created fresh at line 1227, then RestoreTo fails → return err, and `d.parkingNode` remains nil! Then the parked interfaces live in the parking netns but `d.parkingNode` is nil → `EndpointsWithParked()` returns only `d.Endpoints` → CLAB_INTFS undercounts, and next `Start` attempt recreates a new ParkingNode on the same nsPath — `RestoreTo` calls `DiscoverOwnedEndpoints` which discovers owned interfaces in the ns and adopts them... Actually `DiscoverOwnedEndpoints` lists interfaces in the parking netns matching tracked names and adopts runtime endpoints; so a fresh ParkingNode recovers ownership. So the nil `d.parkingNode` on failed restore is mostly recovered by DiscoverOwnedEndpoints next time. Hmm, but is `d.parkingNode` kept on failure? RestoreTo error path: `return err` without `d.parkingNode = parkingNode`. Inconsistent with ParkEndpoints which does keep it on repoint failure. But wait — pre-existing code (RestoreEndpoints not in this PR's diff). This PR only adds EndpointsWithParked. If parkingNode is nil after failed restore but interfaces remain parked, EndpointsWithParked undercounts CLAB_INTFS. But that's an edge of pre-existing code; the PR relies on `d.parkingNode` being set. Marginal — probably not worth flagging since it's pre-existing behavior and the PR didn't worsen it... Actually the PR did make EndpointsWithParked depend on parkingNode state for correctness of CLAB_INTFS. The window: node recreate after a failed start-restore. Pre-existing risk. I'd mention as coverage note maybe.

Now, a more important question: in `EndpointsWithParked`, when parkingNode != nil but CaptureFrom succeeded and then ParkEndpoints returned success — `d.parkingNode = parkingNode` set (line 1209). Good.

Next: the test file. Check imports and the new test compiles: `clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(n, iface, nil)` — check signature. And `NewEndpointVeth`. And `AdoptEndpoint` for DefaultNode exists (line ~1050). `slices.Grow` on `d.Endpoints` — `d.Endpoints` is a field; `d.Endpoints = slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)`. In the test they do `d.Endpoints = slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)` — check the actual test code.

1: package links
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 
7: 	"github.com/containernetworking/plugins/pkg/ns"
8: 	clabnodesstate "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes/state"
9: 	"github.com/vishvananda/netlink"
10: )
11: 
12: type GenericLinkNode struct {
13: 	shortname string
14: 	endpoints []Endpoint
15: 	nspath    string
16: }
17: 
18: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AddLinkToContainer(
19: 	_ context.Context,
20: 	link netlink.Link,
21: 	f func(ns.NetNS) error,
22: ) error {
23: 	// retrieve the namespace handle
24: 	netns, err := ns.GetNS(g.nspath)
25: 	if err != nil {
26: 		return err
27: 	}
28: 	// move veth endpoint to namespace
29: 	if err := netlink.LinkSetNsFd(link, int(netns.Fd())); err != nil {
30: 		return err
31: 	}
32: 	// execute the given function
33: 	return netns.Do(f)
34: }
35: 
36: func (g *GenericLinkNode) ExecFunction(_ context.Context, f func(ns.NetNS) error) error {
37: 	// retrieve the namespace handle
38: 	netns, err := ns.GetNS(g.nspath)
39: 	if err != nil {
40: 		return err
41: 	}
42: 	// execute the given function
43: 	return netns.Do(f)
44: }
45: 
46: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AddEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
47: 	g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)
48: 	return nil
49: }
50: 
51: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AdoptEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
52: 	if e == nil {
53: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q cannot adopt a nil endpoint", g.shortname)
54: 	}
55: 
56: 	owner := e.GetNode()
57: 	if owner == nil {
58: 		return fmt.Errorf(
59: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q without an owner",
60: 			g.shortname,
61: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
62: 		)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	if owner.GetShortName() != g.shortname {
66: 		return fmt.Errorf(
67: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q owned by %q",
68: 			g.shortname,
69: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
70: 			owner.GetShortName(),
71: 		)
72: 	}
73: 
74: 	for _, owned := range g.endpoints {
75: 		if owned == e {
76: 			return nil
77: 		}
78: 		if owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName() {
79: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q already tracks interface %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
80: 		}
81: 	}
82: 
83: 	g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)
84: 	return nil
85: }
86: 
87: func (g *GenericLinkNode) ReleaseEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
88: 	for i, ep := range g.endpoints {
89: 		if ep != e {
90: 			continue
91: 		}
92: 
93: 		g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints[:i], g.endpoints[i+1:]...)
94: 		return nil
95: 	}
96: 
97: 	return fmt.Errorf("node %q does not own endpoint %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
98: }
99: 
100: func (g *GenericLinkNode) GetShortName() string {
101: 	return g.shortname
102: }
103: 
104: func (g *GenericLinkNode) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
105: 	return g.endpoints
106: }
107: 
108: func (*GenericLinkNode) GetState() clabnodesstate.NodeState {
109: 	// The GenericLinkNode is the basis for Mgmt-Bridge and Host fake node.
110: 	// Both of these do generally exist. Hence the Deployed state in generally returned
111: 	return clabnodesstate.Deployed
112: }
113: 
114: func (g *GenericLinkNode) Delete(ctx context.Context) error {
115: 	for _, e := range g.endpoints {
116: 		var err error
117: 		if l := e.GetLink(); l != nil {
118: 			err = l.Remove(ctx)
119: 		} else {
120: 			err = e.Remove(ctx)
121: 		}
122: 		if err != nil {
123: 			return err
124: 		}
125: 	}
126: 	return nil
127: }

g.shortname)\n\t}\n\n\towner := e.GetNode()\n\tif owner == nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q without an 68: func (c *CLab) resolveCopyOutDst(dst string) (string, error) {

96: // copySavedConfig copies the saved configuration file from the path in the SaveConfigResult
97: // to the user-specified destination directory with a timestamp in the filename.
98: // A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to point to the
99: // latest timestamped copy.

104: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config.json                 # symlink -> timestamped copy
105: func (c *CLab) copySavedConfig(
106: 	ctx context.Context,
107: 	result *clabnodes.SaveConfigResult,
108: 	node clabnodes.Node,
109: 	dstRoot string,
110: ) error {
111: 	if result == nil || result.ConfigPath == "" {

133: 			result.ConfigPath, tsPath, err)
134: 	}

138: 	linkPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, fileName)
139: 	_ = os.Remove(linkPath)
140: 	if err := os.Symlink(tsFileName, linkPath); err != nil {
141: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create symlink %q -> %q: %w", linkPath, tsFileName, err)
142: 	}

145: 		"copied saved config",

153: 
154: // timestampedFileName inserts a timestamp before the file extension.
155: // e.g. "config.json" with ts 2026-02-06 23:59:00 UTC -> "config-260206_235900.json".
156: // Files without an extension get the timestamp appended: "startup-config" ->

158: func timestampedFileName(name string, t time.Time) string {
159: 	ts := t.UTC().Format("060102_150405")
160: 	ext := filepath.Ext(name)
161: 	base := strings.TrimSuffix(name, ext)
162:

<path>/source/head/docs/cmd/save.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # save command
2: 
3: ### Description
4: 
5: The `save` command perform configuration save for all the containers running in a lab.
6: 
7: /// note | Clabernetes runtime
8: With `--runtime c9s`, `save` runs the kind-specific save command in each device
9: container. `save --copy` streams the resulting files back to the local
10: destination using the same copy layout as Docker labs. See
11: [Containerlab runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#save) for details.
12: ///
13: 
14: The exact command that performs configuration save depends on a given kind. The below table explains the method used for each kind:
15: 
16: | Kind               | Command                                     | Notes                                                   |
17: | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
18: | **Nokia SR Linux** | `sr_cli -d tools system configuration save` |                                                         |
19: | **Nokia SR OS**    |                                             | delivered via netconf RPC `copy-config running startup` |
20: | **Arista cEOS**    | `Cli -p 15 -c wr`                           |                                                         |
21: | **Juniper cSRX**   | `cli show conf`                             |                                                         |
22: | **Cisco IOL**      | `write memory`                              |                                                         |
23: 
24: --8<-- "docs/cmd/deploy.md:env-vars-flags"
25: 
26: ### Usage
27: 
28: `containerlab [global-flags] save [local-flags]`
29: 
30: ### Flags
31: 
32: #### topology | name
33: 
34: With the global `--topo | -t` flag a user sets the path to the topology definition file that will be used to spin up a lab.
35: 
36: When the topology path refers to a directory, containerlab will look for a file with `.clab.yml` extension in that directory and use it as a topology definition file.
37: 
38: When the topology file flag is omitted, containerlab will try to find the matching file name by looking at the current working directory.
39: 
40: If more than one file is found for directory-based path or when the flag is omitted entirely, containerlab will fail with an error.
41: 
42: Alternatively, use the global `--name` flag to derive the topology from a running lab. This requires the lab to be running and its containers to have the `topo-file` label; otherwise the save command will fail.
43: 
44: #### node-filter
45: 
46: The local `--node-filter` flag allows users to specify a subset of topology nodes targeted by `save` command. The value of this flag is a comma-separated list of node names as they appear in the topology.
47: 
48: When a subset of nodes is specified, containerlab will only attempt to save configuration on the selected nodes.
49: 
50: #### copy
51: 
52: The local `--copy` flag allows users to copy saved configuration files to a dedicated directory. The path is resolved relative to the current working directory unless an absolute path is provided.
53: 
54: When `--copy` is specified, containerlab copies the saved configuration file from its original location in the lab directory to the destination with a UTC timestamp embedded in the filename. A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to always point to the latest timestamped copy.
55: 
56: ```
57: <copy-path>/clab-<labname>/<node-name>/<config>-<YYMMDD_HHMMSS>.<ext>   # timestamped copy
58: <copy-path>/clab-<labname>/<node-name>/<config>.<ext>                    # symlink → latest
59: ```
60: 
61: The destination directory is created automatically if it does not exist. Running `save --copy` multiple times to the same directory preserves all previous saves, allowing easy rollback to an earlier configuration.
62: 
63: The exact file that is copied depends on the node kind and corresponds to the configuration file produced by the save operation:
64: 
65: | Kind               | Copied file                                  |
66: | ------------------ | -------------------------------------------- |
67: | **Nokia SR Linux** | `config/config.json`                         |
68: | **Nokia SR OS**    | `<slot>/config/cf3/config.cfg`               |
69: | **Arista cEOS**    | `flash/startup-config`                       |
70: | **Juniper cSRX**   | `config/juniper.conf`                        |
71: 
72: Node kinds that do not report a saved config path are silently skipped.
73: 
74: The `copy` flag opens up the path for saving the running configuration that is used for the startup config. By using the [magic variables](../manual/topo-def-file.md#magic-variables) in the topology file, you can point the startup config to the saved configuration without statically hardcoding the path.  
75: Consider the following lab:
76: 
77: ```yaml
78: name: mylab
79: topology:
80:   nodes:
81:     node1:
82:     node2:
83: ```
84: 
85: After running the lab, save the configuration to a directory:
86: 
87: ```bash
88: containerlab save --copy startup-configs -t mylab.clab.yml
89: ```
90: 
91: The saved configuration will be in the `startup-configs` directory which you can now specify as the startup config for the nodes of the same topology using `__clabLabName__` and `__clabNodeName__` magic variables:
92: 
93: ```yaml
94: name: mylab
95: topology:
96:   nodes:
97:     node1:
98:       startup-config: startup-configs/__clabLabName__/__clabNodeName__/config.json
99:     node2:
100:       startup-config: startup-configs/__clabLabName__/__clabNodeName__/config.json
101: ```
102: 
103: ### Examples
104: 
105: #### Save the configuration of the containers in a specific lab
106: 
107: Save the configuration of the containers running in lab named srl02
108: 
109: ```bash
110: ❯ containerlab save --name srl02
111: INFO[0001] clab-srl02-srl1: stdout: /system:
112:     Generated checkpoint '/etc/opt/srlinux/checkpoint/checkpoint-0.json' with name 'checkpoint-2020-11-18T09:00:54.998Z' and comment ''
113: 
114: INFO[0002] clab-srl02-srl2: stdout: /system:
115:     Generated checkpoint '/etc/opt/srlinux/checkpoint/checkpoint-0.json' with name 'checkpoint-2020-11-18T09:00:56.444Z' and comment ''
116: ```
117: 
118: #### Save and copy configs to a reusable directory
119: 
120: ```bash
121: ❯ containerlab save -t srl02.clab.yml --copy ./startup-configs
122: ```
123: 
124: This creates timestamped copies with symlinks pointing to the latest save:
125: 
126: ```
127: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl1/config-260207_091500.json   # timestamped copy
128: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl1/config.json                 # symlink → config-260207_091500.json
129: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl2/config-260207_091500.json
130: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl2/config.json
131: ```
132: 
133: Running the same command again creates new timestamped files and updates the symlinks, while the previous saves remain in the directory.

(End of file - total 133 lines)
</content>

Reasoning
No save_test.go in the snapshot. Note: the docs/cmd/save.md is in the snapshot's head inventory — and it's not in the changed list, so no doc update was made for the error behavior change. Is the error behavior change user-visible enough to require docs? `clab save` previously succeeded (exit 0) with logged errors; now fails with joined error. Users scripting `clab save` will see exit 1. This is arguably an improvement; docs don't state exit behavior either way. I'd call it a minor note (P4 or coverage note), not necessarily a finding. Actually the failure mode: partial saves still completed for nodes that succeeded — the command now exits non-zero while some saves did happen. That's reasonable.

Now check whether `errors.Join` message formatting collides with prior log behavior — the per-node error message remains similar.

One thing to verify in `Save`: the loop appends `nil` then spawns goroutine that writes `errs[i]`. All goroutines write to distinct indices of a pre-allocated slice — safe. But note `errs` slice header is captured by goroutines; the slice is not reallocated after the loop since capacity == len(c.Nodes) and we append exactly len(c.Nodes) times... wait, cap is len(c.Nodes), appends = len(c.Nodes), so no reallocation. Even if reallocation occurred, goroutines hold their own slice header pointing to the old array with index i — writes to old array would be lost only if a realloc happened mid-loop before all appends. But appends happen synchronously in the loop before... Actually appends happen while goroutines may be running (spawned earlier in the loop). If a realloc happened at append k, previously spawned goroutines hold headers pointing to the old array — their writes to index i<k would be in the old array, lost! But cap = len(c.Nodes) preallocated, and we append exactly len(c.Nodes) times, so no realloc ever happens. Safe. But it's fragile: if someone later changes capacity, subtle race. Hmm — is this worth flagging? The code is correct as written; the invariant (cap == number of appends) holds. A simpler and more robust alternative: use a channel or index by node order. But per review standards, flagging speculative robustness is unnecessary. It's correct. Skip.

Wait, actually one more check: `errs` cap is `len(c.Nodes)`, appends exactly `len(c.Nodes)`. Yes, no realloc. Fine.

Next: does the joined error get used anywhere else expecting nil semantics? `cmd/save.go` returns it. Fine.

Now check `nodes/default_node.go` for `GetEndpoints` in interface — is `EndpointsWithParked` needed on other kinds that embed DefaultNode but have their own Deploy setting CLAB_INTFS? We grepped; only default_node and linux. But other kinds might override `Deploy` and call DefaultNode.Deploy — fine.

Now — the more subtle issue: `EndpointsWithParked` is only defined on `*DefaultNode`. Is there any node kind that sets CLAB_INTFS or uses NETMAP-like config via a different implementation (e.g., vr-sros uses sros.go with its own Deploy)? They weren't counted before either; not worsened.

Now, check `iol.go` around Deploy — does IOL override Deploy? Grep "func (n *iol) Deploy".

56: func TestDefaultNodeEndpointsWithParked(t *testing.T) {
57: 	newNode := func() *DefaultNode {
58: 		return &DefaultNode{Cfg: &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "node1", LongName: "clab-test-node1"}}
59: 	}
60: 	veth := func(n clablinks.Node, iface string) clablinks.Endpoint {
61: 		return clablinks.NewEndpointVeth(clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(n, iface, nil))
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	tests := []struct {
65: 		name   string
66: 		owned  []string
67: 		parked []string // nil means no parking node
68: 		want   []string
69: 	}{
70: 		{name: "no parking node", owned: []string{"eth1", "eth2"}, want: []string{"eth1", "eth2"}},
71: 		{name: "all parked for recreate", owned: nil, parked: []string{"eth1", "eth2"}, want: []string{"eth1", "eth2"}},
72: 		{
73: 			name:   "owned and parked split after a failed restore",
74: 			owned:  []string{"eth3"},
75: 			parked: []string{"eth1", "eth2"},
76: 			want:   []string{"eth3", "eth1", "eth2"},
77: 		},
78: 	}
79: 	for _, tt := range tests {
80: 		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
81: 			d := newNode()
82: 			for _, iface := range tt.owned {
83: 				if err := d.AdoptEndpoint(veth(d, iface)); err != nil {
84: 					t.Fatalf("adopt %s: %v", iface, err)
85: 				}
86: 			}
87: 			if tt.parked != nil {
88: 				d.parkingNode = clablinks.NewParkingNode(d.Cfg.LongName, "/run/netns/test")
89: 				for _, iface := range tt.parked {
90: 					if err := d.parkingNode.AddEndpoint(veth(d.parkingNode, iface)); err != nil {
91: 						t.Fatalf("park %s: %v", iface, err)
92: 					}
93: 				}
94: 			}
95: 
96: 			// Spare capacity lets a missing clone show up as aliasing.
97: 			d.Endpoints = slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)
98: 
99: 			got := d.EndpointsWithParked()
100: 			var names []string
101: 			for _, ep := range got {
102: 				names = append(names, ep.GetIfaceName())
103: 			}
104: 			if !slices.Equal(names, tt.want) {
105: 				t.Fatalf("got %v, want %v", names, tt.want)
106: 			}
107: 
108: 			// The result must not alias d.Endpoints when parked endpoints are added,
109: 			// or appending to it would corrupt the node's own list.

188: +				}
189: +			}

192: +				for _, iface := range tt.parked {
193: +					if err := d.parkingNode.AddEndpoint(veth(d.parkingNode, iface)); err != nil {
194: +						t.Fatalf("park %s: %v", iface, err)
195: +					}
196: +				}
197: +			}
198: +
199: +			// Spare capacity lets a missing clone show up as aliasing.
200: +			d.Endpoints = slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)
201: +
202: +			got := d.EndpointsWithParked()
203: +			var names []string
204: +			for _, ep := range got {
205: +				names = append(names, ep.GetIfaceName())
206: +			}
207: +			if !slices.Equal(names, tt.want) {
208: +				t.Fatalf("got %v, want %v", names, tt.want)
209: +			}
210: +
211: +			// The result must not alias d.Endpoints when parked endpoints are added,
212: +			// or appending to it would corrupt the node's own list.
213: +			if tt.parked != nil && len(d.Endpoints) > 0 && &got[0] == &d.Endpoints[0] {
214: +				t.Fatal("result aliases d.Endpoints")
215: +			}
216: +			if len(d.Endpoints) != len(tt.owned) {
217: +				t.Fatalf("d.Endpoints changed: got %d, want %d", len(d.Endpoints), len(tt.owned))
218: +			}
219: +		})

Reasoning
All hunks verified now. Let me check `iol.Deploy` — iol doesn't override Deploy, so `DefaultNode.Deploy` sets CLAB_INTFS with parked count. Good.

Now let me double-check the IOL recreate flow in `apply`:

1. `parkRecreatedNodes` → for IOL recreated nodes, `ParkEndpoints` moves all endpoints (including mgmt? no — mgmt endpoints are not in d.Endpoints; they're mgmt-net specific). Data endpoints move to parkingNode; `d.Endpoints` emptied.

2. Recreate: `deployNode` → `PreDeploy` → for IOL: `CreateIOLFiles` → `GenInterfaceConfig` with `EndpointsWithParked()` (all parked endpoints present) → NETMAP correct. Also `ensureNumSlotsEnv` → CLAB_IOL_NUMSLOTS correct.

3. `Deploy` (DefaultNode.Deploy) → CLAB_INTFS = total (owned + parked) → correct.

4. `RestoreEndpoints` → parked interfaces moved from parking netns into new container netns, `d.Endpoints` repopulated via AdoptEndpoint.

This all hangs together. Now some edge considerations:

A. During recreate of an IOL node, the NEW container is created, but the endpoints are restored AFTER Deploy. The vrnetlab-style IOL container uses CLAB_INTFS to postpone startup until all interfaces present. With this PR, CLAB_INTFS counts parked interfaces, which will be added shortly. Good.

B. But wait — `PostDeploy` for IOL calls `UpdateMgmtIntf` which uses `n.mgmtIntf`/eth0 mapping. Not endpoint-dependent.

C. `GenInterfaceConfig` writes NETMAP entries for parked endpoints. The parked endpoints have `GetIfaceName()` preserved (interface names don't change when parked). So NETMAP lines will reference ethX names matching what will be restored. Good.

D. One subtle thing: `iolPortForLinuxIdx` uses `intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName())` — for parked endpoints with names like "eth1" fine.

E. Now, what about `d.parkingNode.GetEndpoints()` — `NewParkingNode(longName, path)` returns `*ParkingNode` — check its type: it's `*clablinks.ParkingNode` embedding `*GenericLinkNode`? Let me verify ParkingNode struct embeds GenericLinkNode so GetEndpoints works. I read parking_node.go earlier (per summary) — yes it presumably embeds GenericLinkNode.

Reasoning
The docs structure is valid mkdocs-material nesting (tab → warning → close → close).

Now let me consider findings:

1. `core/save.go`: behavior change — `clab save` now exits non-zero when a node save fails. This is arguably a fix (failures were previously silently swallowed). Docs don't state exit behavior; no doc update needed per se. Not a finding; could be a coverage note.

2. Test for save error aggregation: none added. Ponytail rule: "non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind". This change involves concurrent error collection. A unit test with fake nodes could verify (a) all errors joined, (b) nil when all succeed. However, `c.Nodes` and node.SaveConfig are concrete — a test would need a fake node implementing clabnodes.Node interface, which is heavy. Is there an existing pattern? `core/apply_test.go` exists per earlier note — check if it has fake nodes. Actually earlier I noted `core/apply_test.go` with fakes. If a fake node type exists in core tests, a small save test is feasible. But maybe the Save path is hard to test since it needs CLab instance. Hmm — the P4 recommendation: add a focused test reusing existing fakes, or note the gap. Let me check what fakes exist in core tests.

3. Potential issue in `Save`: `errs` indexed per node — fine. `errors.Join` returns nil if all nil. Good.

4. `EndpointsWithParked`: correct, tested. One design note: when `parkingNode == nil` it returns the internal slice directly (aliased) — callers only use it read-only (`len`, `range`), and the test asserts non-aliasing only in the parked case. Fine.

5. Docs: telnet to `<container-name/id> 5000` — established repo convention (dell_sonic, plvision_sonic, openbsd, generic_vm, vr-ftdv, vr-ros all use this pattern). The claim "served on the node's management address" — I can't verify vrnetlab-side; it's plausible. If CreateFile appends... unlikely. Let me verify CreateFile semantics quickly to be safe.

9. Now, one genuinely worth-checking item: in `deployNode` (core/deploy.go 789-809), the order is PreDeploy → Deploy → UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo. For recreated parked nodes, Deploy creates the container with CLAB_INTFS counting parked — but the actual interfaces get attached in RestoreEndpoints AFTER Deploy. So CLAB_INTFS is "expected count", which matches vrnetlab's semantics (postpone startup until all interfaces added). This is precisely the fix.
